Each *qīla* (saying) unmasks a *qālū* (saying-back) — counsel as X-ray. This verse's X-ray shows hollow: words in, nothing out. Silence-as-answer convicts: the counseled who will not even *reply* have replied.

**Ittaqū Mā Bayna Aydīkum: "Be Mindful of That Before You"**

*"Be mindful of that which is before you"* — *ittaqū mā bayna aydīkum* — *ittaqū* (be mindful, guard, fear-protectively — *taqwā*'s imperative): the Qur'an's master-virtue commanded. *Taqwā* (as the Analogies teach: carefully walking/driving a dangerous path — awareness preventing harm) is *precautionary piety*: seeing peril, taking cover. *"That before you"* (*mā bayna aydīkum* — "what is between your hands," i.e., ahead): the commentators (al-Qurṭubī, al-Ṭabarsī; Mujāhid, Ibn ʿAbbās via Ibn Kathīr) unfold layers. *The Hereafter ahead*: judgment, Fire, Garden (vv. 53–64) — be mindful *now* of what stands *before* (future). *Sins committed*: past deeds' consequences advancing toward thee (v. 12's *qaddamū*, sent-forth, awaiting). *The visible/apparent*: present perils (Cry, v. 49; drowning, v. 43; destruction, v. 31) — mindfulness of evident warnings. All three face *forward*: *taqwā* as forward-looking caution — the driver's eyes on road-ahead (potholes, cliffs, destination). The Qur'an's *taqwā*-promise crowns the counsel: *"that haply you may receive mercy"* (*laʿallakum turḥamūn*) — mindfulness *earns* mercy (v. 44's *raḥmah* made conditional-upon-heeding). *Laʿalla* (that haply) preserves freedom: mercy *offered*, not imposed — the mindful *may* receive (and God never disappoints the mindful — 39:61: *"God delivers the mindful"*). Behold the road-ahead — and brake.

**Wa Mā Khalfakum: "And of That Behind You"**

*"And of that which is behind you"* — *wa mā khalfakum* — mindfulness's rear-view mirror. *"Behind"* (*khalf*): the commentators' layers mirror the front. *This world behind*: life's delusions to *leave* unmourned (v. 44's *matāʿ*, enjoyment-for-while — beware its seduction even as it recedes). *Sins to come*: future wrongs to *avoid* (Ibn ʿAbbās: past sins = *"before"* as consequences-advancing; future sins = *"behind"* as temptations-trailing — either direction, mindfulness covers all). *The hidden*: unseen perils (veiled hearts, vv. 8–9; sudden Cry, v. 49; death's stealth) — *taqwā* of the invisible-behind (the past's unrepented, the future's unguarded). The front-behind pair (*bayna aydīkum... khalfakum*) echoes the barriers-verse (v. 9: *"barrier before them and behind"*) — inverted: there, barriers *imposed* (judgment); here, mindfulness *commanded* (prevention). Heed *before-behind* now (v. 45), or be *walled* before-behind then (v. 9). The Qur'an's directional *taqwā* recurs: *"consider what is before and behind of sky and earth"* (34:9 — cosmic front-behind); *"He knows what is before and behind them"* (2:255 — divine omniscience encompassing both). Man's mindfulness must *mirror* God's knowledge directionally: He *knows* all around; thou must *heed* all around. Front (future-judgment, past-deeds'-fruits) and behind (world's lures, hidden perils) — *taqwā* all-around, mercy all-encompassing (*laʿallakum turḥamūn*).

**Laʿallakum Turḥamūn: Mercy as Mindfulness's Wage**

*"That haply you may receive mercy"* — mercy (*turḥamūn*, be-mercied) as *taqwā*'s *purpose*-clause: mindfulness → mercy. The Qur'an's mercy-economy is consistent: *"My mercy embraces all things, and I shall prescribe it for the mindful"* (7:156) — embrace universal, prescription conditional. *Laʿalla* (haply) is mercy's etiquette: God *may* (certainly, by promise — yet freely, by majesty). The counsel's structure (command → promise) models all *daʿwah* (invitation): *ittaqū* (do) *laʿalla* (receive) — duty with incentive, never bare threat. The runner (vv. 20–25) practiced it (follow → saved); the sūrah's close will too (worship → Garden, vv. 55–58). For Makkah's hearers, the promise pricked: mercy *offered* (still!), mercy *conditioned* (mindfulness!), mercy *delayed* (haply — decide!). The verse's unspoken sequel (v. 46: turning-away) makes the offer tragic: mercy extended, backs turned. Yet the offer *stands* — every recitation re-extends it: *be mindful (now) — mercy (haply, assuredly)*. The mindful-then (Companions) received mercy-then (victories, Garden-promises); the mindful-now receive mercy-now (tranquillity, provision, forgiveness) and mercy-after (*"Peace!"* v. 58). *Ittaqū* — and *turḥamūn*.

**Mindfulness Front-and-Behind in Daily Practice**

The verse's *taqwā*-bidirectional becomes a daily examen. *Front-mindfulness (before)*: each morning, face the day-ahead mindfully — schedule prayers (appointments with the Tribunal-preview, vv. 32, 53), anticipate temptations (meetings, screens, angers — pre-decide righteousness), remember death-possible-today (v. 49's *"while they dispute"* — disputes killable mid-sentence). Each night, face the future-ahead: Hereafter (recite vv. 51–64 as bedtime-preview), deeds'-fruits (v. 12's registry entries today — audit), resolutions (tomorrow's *taqwā*-targets). *Behind-mindfulness*: each evening, review the day-behind — sins committed (repent specifically — *istighfār* with naming), duties missed (make up — *qaḍāʾ*), blessings received (thank — *shukr*, v. 35). Periodically, review life-behind: unrepented pasts (major sins — repentUpdate: conditions: abandon, remorse, resolve, restore-rights), trailing habits (address), world's receding lures (detach — v. 44's *matāʿ* loosening). *Mercy-pursuit*: link every mindfulness to mercy-seeking — after each *taqwā*-act, ask *raḥmah* (*"O Merciful, mercy me as I mind Thee"*). For the verse's final mercy is its *qīla* (it-is-said) — counsel *still spoken*, to *thee*, *now*. The deniers' response (omitted — they turned, v. 46) need not be thine. *When it is said unto thee* — this verse, this reading — *be mindful* (front: judgment nears; behind: sins trail) — *that thou mayest receive mercy*. Say, inwardly: *samiʿnā wa aṭaʿnā* (we hear and obey, 2:285) — and turn the omitted-response into mercy-received. *Ittaqū* — today. *Turḥamūn* — tomorrow, and Then.

---
## Sūrah Yā Sīn 36:46

> **Never did a sign from among the signs of their Lord come unto them but that they turned away from it.**

**Expanded Commentary**

**Wa Mā Taʾtīhim Min Āyah: The Universal Turning**

*"Never did a sign... come unto them but that they turned away"* — *wa mā taʾtīhim min āyatin... illā kānū ʿanhā muʿriḍīn* — *mā... illā* (never... but): exceptionless. *Taʾtīhim* (comes to them): signs *arrive* — heaven delivers (earth's revival, v. 33; night's flaying, v. 37; sun-moon, vv. 38–40; arks, vv. 41–42; the town's parable, vv. 13–30; this Qur'an, v. 2). *Muʿriḍīn* (turning away): backs presented to arrivals — the counsel's omitted-response (v. 45) now explicit: *turning*. The verse parallels:

> *"And never does a sign from their Lord's signs come to them but they turn away from it."* (Qur'an 6:4)

— twin verses, twin peoples (Makkan deniers here and there), twin verdicts. *"From among the signs of their Lord"* (*min āyāti rabbihim*): *their* Lord — the signs' Sender is *theirs* (Creator, Owner — v. 22's *rabbikum*, your Lord) — turning from *His* signs is turning from *one's own*. The signs' scope (commentators): preceding verses' nature-signs, creation entire, revelation entire — *"all are signs for those possessed of intellect"* (3:190). Turning's *universality* (*never... but*) diagnoses not this-or-that dismissal but *dismissal-as-policy*: the town (vv. 15, 18), Quraysh (mockery, v. 30), every *qarn* (v. 31) — arrivals constant, backs constant. *Kānū* (they were/kept): turning as *habit*, settled character — not lapse but lifestyle. Signs knock; lifestyle bolts.

**Iʿrāḍ: The Anatomy of Turning Away**

*Iʿrāḍ* (turning away) is the Qur'an's verb for heedlessness-in-motion: not static ignorance (v. 6's *ghāfilūn*) but *active* aversion — face averted, steps redirected, attention withdrawn. Its anatomy unfolds across revelation. *Stage one, aversion*: *"who does greater wrong than one reminded of his Lord's signs, then turns away and forgets what his hands sent forth?"* (18:57) — reminder → turning → forgetting (v. 12's registry ignored). *Stage two, sealing*: *"We placed coverings over their hearts, lest they understand, and deafness in ears"* (18:57) — turning *earns* covering (v. 9's veiling as wage). *"Even if thou callest them to guidance, they will never be rightly guided"* (18:57) — chronic turning becomes terminal. *Stage three, deserving*: *"I shall turn away from My signs those who wax arrogant without right. Even if they saw every sign, they would not believe"* (7:146) — God's *turning* answers man's: aversion reciprocated. The progression (turn → covered → turned-from) mirrors vv. 6–10 (heedless → Word-due → shackled → walled → warning-equals-silence). *Iʿrāḍ* is thus the *hinge-sin*: each turning tightens the veil (v. 9), adds a link (v. 8), nears the Cry (v. 49). The verse's *never... but* warns: turning *once* is lapse (repentable — 39:53); turning *always* is *iʿrāḍ*-character (the verse's people). Between lapses, return; before character sets, repent. Signs still arrive — *face* them.

**Why Signs Cannot Compel: The Veil Behind the Turning**

The verse raises revelation's epistemological mystery: if signs are *clear* (vv. 33–44's gallery — undeniable revivals, flayings, orbits), why do men turn? The Qur'an's answer is volitional-veiling: *"It is not that these people cannot see the signs, but that God veils them due to their arrogance and disbelief"* (source commentary on 7:146). Arrogance (*istikbār*) precedes veiling: the town's chin-up (v. 8), Pharaoh's *"better than this despicable"* (43:52), Quraysh's *"shall we abandon gods for a mad poet?"* (37:36) — pride *first*, blindness *following*. The mechanism is observable: pride filters perception — the arrogant cannot *afford* signs' conclusion (submission), so perception *edits* arrivals (coincidence, nature, myth). Each edit thickens the veil (v. 9's *aghshaynā*, We veiled) until *muʿriḍīn* (turning) becomes *lā yubṣirūn* (see-not, v. 9). God's veiling is thus *judicial confirmation* of self-veiling: *"so when they deviated, God caused hearts to deviate"* (61:5). The mercy within severity: veiling follows *persistent* turning (*kānū*, kept-turning) — not first aversion. Early turnings meet renewed signs (v. 14's third messenger; vv. 33–44's gallery-after-gallery) — heaven *multiplies* arrivals before confirming departures. The verse's *never... but* describes the *end* of long refusal, not its start. At *thy* stage (reading this verse = sign arriving), turning remains *optional*. *Face* it — and veils lift ( *"God confirms believers with the firm word,"* 14:27).

**The Signs They Turned From: Yā Sīn's Gallery Indicting**

The verse's *"signs"* (*āyāt*) in context indicts specifically Yā Sīn's displayed gallery — each turned-from sign a prosecution-exhibit. Exhibit earth (vv. 33–35): revival-eaten-yet-denied (resurrection doubted while bread chewed). Exhibit night (v. 37): death-slept-yet-denied (dawn-risings witnessed, final Rising doubted). Exhibit sun-moon (vv. 38–40): orbits-navigated-yet-unthanked (calendars used, Measurer ignored). Exhibit arks (vv. 41–44): voyages-landed-yet-unmercified (deliverances enjoyed, Deliverer unpraised). Exhibit counsel (v. 45): mindfulness-urged-yet-unheeded (front-behind warned, backs turned). Exhibit parable (vv. 13–30): town-extinguished-yet-unconsidered (ruins passed, lessons unlearned — v. 31). Six exhibits, one response: *iʿrāḍ* (turning). The prosecution's closing (vv. 48–54) follows: scoffing (v. 47), mock-timing (v. 48), Cry (v. 49), no-bequest (v. 50), raising (v. 51), confession (v. 52), arraignment (v. 53), verdict (v. 54: *"recompensed save for what you did"* — including turnings). The gallery's abundance *is* the severity: much-given, much-required (*"to whom much is given, much is required"* — cf. 5:115's severest-punishment for sign-rejecters after the Table). Yā Sīn's readers receive *more* signs per page than most peoples per century — privilege as peril. *Much arrived; much answers.* Turn — or face. The exhibits await thy plea.

**Facing Signs: Practices of the Non-Turning**

The verse's *muʿriḍīn* (turning) inverts into disciplines of *iqbāl* (facing). *Face nature-signs*: schedule creation-contemplation — dawn-watching (v. 37's flaying-reversed), garden-walking (vv. 33–35's revival), night-skying (vv. 38–40's orbits), sea-voyaging (vv. 41–44's carriage) — each with verses recited *to* subjects. The Prophet ﷺ faced signs verbally (*duʿās* for new moon, thunder, wind, rain) — imitate: vocalize awe (*subḥāna*, v. 36) at each witnessing. *Face scripture-signs*: recite *facing* (heart-toward, not duty-past) — pause at each *āyah* (sign-verse): *what arrives? what responds?* Journal responses (convictions, repentances, gratitudes) — arrivals logged, turnings prevented. Study *asbāb* (occasions), *tafsīr* (commentaries — Ṭabarī, Kathīr, Qurṭubī), cross-references (this commentary's method) — depth prevents deflection. *Face counsel-signs*: when *qīla* (it-is-said, v. 45) arrives — sermon, friend's advice, child's question, enemy's barb-with-truth — *face* (listen fully, weigh honestly, obey promptly) rather than *turn* (deflect, delay, dismiss). The righteous predecessors *sought* counsel (*"the believer is his brother's mirror"*) — turning's opposite. *Unveil progressively*: where turning has begun (dullness to verses, yawns at sermons, irritation at advisers), *reverse* deliberately — increase exposure (more recitation, more company-of-reminder, more solitude-reflection) before veiling (v. 9) confirms. For the verse's final mercy is its *present* (*taʾtīhim*, comes — ongoing): signs *still* arrive — this verse among them, arriving *now*. The *muʿriḍīn* turned from arrivals; thou mayest *arrive* at turning's end — by facing. *Face* — and be faced (*"God comes to the walker,"* v. 8's ḥadīth qudsī). The signs knock. *Open.*

---
## Sūrah Yā Sīn 36:47

> **And when it is said unto them, "Spend of that wherewith God has provided you," those who disbelieve say to those who believe, "Are we to feed one whom, if God willed, He would feed him? You are in naught but manifest error."**

**Expanded Commentary**

**Anfiqū Mimmā Razaqakumu Allāh: The Command to Spend**

*"Spend of that wherewith God has provided you"* — *anfiqū mimmā razaqakumu Allāh* — *anfiqū* (spend, expend — *infāq*'s imperative): wealth's *outflow* commanded. *Mimmā* (of that which): *from* provision, not *all* (proportionate giving — zakāh's 2.5%, ṣadaqah's generosity). *Razaqakumu Allāh* (God provided you): provision's *source* named — God *provided*; thou *spendest* — stewardship's grammar: gifts received (vv. 33–35's grain-gardens, vv. 71–73's cattle) must *flow* (to poor, kin, wayfarer). The command's occasion (al-Qurṭubī): Quraysh's disbelievers scoffed at Abū Bakr, who "used to feed the destitute among the Muslims from his own wealth" — they asked, "Dost thou claim God can feed these?" — "Yes" — "Why feeds He them not?" — "The poor are commanded patience, the wealthy commanded giving" — "Thou art in error...!" — and this verse (with 92:5–6) descended. The command thus defends *Abū Bakr's* charity (and all believers') against *scoffers'* theology — revelation siding with givers against mockers. *Anfiqū* echoes the Qur'an's constant imperative (*"spend of what We provided before death comes,"* 63:10; *"the likeness of spenders... seven ears, hundred grains,"* 2:261) — spending as faith's *proof* (zakāh = purification; ṣadaqah from *ṣidq*, truthfulness — charity *proves* belief true). The commanded (disbelievers — *"when said unto them"*) refuse; the commended (believers — Abū Bakr) spend. Spending divides (v. 59's *"stand apart"*) before Judgment divides.

**A-Nuṭʿimu Man Law Yashāʾu Allāh: The Scoffers' Pseudo-Theology**

*"Are we to feed one whom, if God willed, He would feed him?"* — *a-nuṭʿimu man law yashāʾu Allāhu aṭʿamah* — the scoff: *if* God *willed* (*law yashāʾ*) the poor fed, *He* would feed (*aṭʿamah*) — since He hasn't, *we* shan't. The logic *weaponizes* decree (*qadar*): God's will as excuse for man's withholding — theology as miserliness's mask. The fallacy is twofold. *First, will-confusion*: God's *cosmic* will (permitting poverty — as trial) ≠ God's *legislative* will (commanding feeding — as duty). He *wills* (permits) hunger *that* men *will* (choose) feeding — poverty as the wealthy's *examination* (Abū Bakr's answer: poor-tried-by-patience, wealthy-by-giving), not the poor's *verdict*. The scoffers collapse the two wills: *permitted-therefore-commanded-to-ignore* — as if disease's permission canceled medicine's duty, or ignorance's existence excused teaching. *Second, agency-denial*: God feeds *through* men — *"We feed you"* (76:8–9's righteous: *"we feed you for God's Face"*) — human hands as divine catering. To demand God feed *without* men is to demand harvests without farmers (56:64's Grower-uses-sowers), guidance without messengers (v. 14's sending), rescue without runners (v. 20). *Law yashāʾ* (if He willed) is true (He *could* feed directly — manna, 2:57) yet irrelevant (He *willed* feeding-through-thee — *anfiqū*). The scoff is answered by its own premise: *yes* — God *wills* him fed — *through thee*. Feed — and fulfill the will thou citest.

**"You Are in Naught but Manifest Error": Accusation Inverted**

*"You are in naught but manifest error"* — *in antum illā fī ḍalālin mubīn* — the scoffers hurl *ḍalāl mubīn* (manifest error — v. 24's phrase) at *believers*: charity as straying, generosity as delusion. The inversion is total: the runner (v. 24) called *idolatry* manifest error; scoffers call *charity* it. Truth's vocabulary in mockers' mouths (v. 30's *yastahziʾūn*, mocking) — praise-as-blame, light-as-darkness (*"woe to those who call evil good,"* cf. 41:34's context). Psychologically, the accusation *projects*: the *withholding* (true *ḍalāl* — miserliness condemned, 3:180: *"let not the miserly suppose... it is evil for them"*) accuse the *giving*. Projection protects conscience: call givers misguided, and withholding feels wise. The Qur'an reverses the reversal: *"Satan threatens you with poverty and commands indecency; God promises forgiveness and bounty"* (2:268) — the scoff's voice identified (Satan's), the giver's vindicated (God's promise). Al-Rāzī notes the eschatological irony: *ḍalāl mubīn* (manifest error) will be *manifested* (made-evident, v. 24's *mubīn*-active) at judgment — givers *manifestly* rewarded (v. 11's *ajr karīm*, generous reward; 2:261's 700-fold), withholders *manifestly* punished (3:180's collared-hoards; 104:2–3's hoarder in *Ḥuṭamah*). Accusations await manifestation: hurl *ḍalāl* now; wear it then — or shed it now (by spending) and shine then. The manifest will manifest.

**Decree and Duty: The Verse's Theological Crux**

The verse is revelation's textbook on *qadar* (decree) versus *amr* (command) — and its resolution governs all fatalism. *Principle*: decree *describes* (what God permits/wills cosmically), command *prescribes* (what men must do legislatively) — never confuse. Poverty's *existence* (decreed) never cancels feeding's *obligation* (commanded); the sick's *predestination* never excuses medicine; the damned's *foreknowledge* (v. 7's Word-due) never excuses warning (v. 6). The Prophet ﷺ settled it: asked whether medicine/prophylaxis repel decree, he said, *"They are part of God's decree"* (al-Tirmidhī 2148; Ibn Mājah 3437, from Ibn ʿAbbās; graded ḥasan) — means *are* decree's vehicles, not rivals. Asked why act if fates are written, he said, *"Act — each is facilitated toward his created-for"* (v. 7's ḥadīth, al-Bukhārī 4949) — facilitation *through* action. The scoffers' error (fatalism-as-excuse) recurs perennially: *"why pray if guided-or-not is written?"* — pray, for prayer *is* the written-path of the guided. *"Why work if provision is apportioned?"* — work, for work *is* apportionment's vehicle (v. 35's *"their hands have worked"* honoured). *"Why warn if Word-is-due?"* (v. 7) — warn, for warning *is* duty (v. 17's *balāgh*), outcome God's. *Qadar* consoles (after calamity — *"God decreed"*), never excuses (before duty). The scoffer inverts: excuses-before, despairs-after. The believer: strives-before (*anfiqū*!), trusts-after (*qadar*!). *That* is the verse's theology — in two directions.

**Spending Against Scoffing: Practices of the Giver**

The verse mobilizes giving under fire. *Give commandedly*: spend (*anfiqū*) as *amr* (order), not option — zakāh calculatedly (purify annually), ṣadaqah habitually (daily, even little — *"shield yourselves from Fire though with half a date,"* al-Bukhārī 1417; Muslim 1016, from ʿAdī), hospitality reflexively (feed — Abū Bakr's model). Give *mimmā razaqakum* (from God's provision) — acknowledging Source (no pride), proportion (no extravagance-harm — 17:29's neither-shackled-nor-outspread), priority (kin, poor, wayfarer, debtor — 2:177's order). *Answer scoffers Abū-Bakrly*: when mocked for giving ("why feed whom God left hungry?"), reply his theology — *poor-tried-by-patience, wealthy-by-giving* — and his practice (keep feeding). Never debate decree with withholders (their *law-yashāʾ* is shield, not search); *demonstrate* duty (thy giving *is* God's willing-through-thee). *Expect inversion*: givers *will* be called misguided (*ḍalāl mubīn*, v. 47; "wasteful," "enablers," "fools") — wear the accusation as the runner wore stones (v. 26: praying-while-stoned) — vindication manifests (v. 24's *mubīn*-active) at *muḥḍarūn* (vv. 32, 53). *Give eschatologically*: each coin a *qaddamū* (sent-forth, v. 12) awaiting thee; each meal a *matāʿ*-to-*ajr* conversion (v. 44's expiry into v. 11's eternity). For the verse's final mercy is its *recording*: scoffers' sneers recorded (v. 12), givers' gifts recorded (v. 12) — both *muḥḍar* (produced, v. 32). Abū Bakr's loaves outlasted scoffers' laughs — recited here, rewarded There (v. 11's *ajr karīm*). *Spend* — though scoffed. *Feed* — though called erring. The Fed (poor) will testify; the Feeder (God) will repay — *manifestly*.

---
## Sūrah Yā Sīn 36:48

> **And they say, "When will this promise come to pass, if you are truthful?"**

**Expanded Commentary**

**Matā Hādhā al-Waʿd: The Mockers' Timetable Demand**

*"When will this promise come to pass?"* — *matā hādhā al-waʿdu* — *matā* (when?): the deniers demand *timing* for the Hour/Resurrection's *promise* (*waʿd* — the threatened-then-promised Day, vv. 49–54). *"If you are truthful"* (*in kuntum ṣādiqīn*): sincerity *conditioned* on scheduling — truthfulness *measured* by date-giving. The challenge recurs across revelation (10:48; 21:38; 27:71; 34:29; 67:25) — stock-scoff, every prophet's hecklers: Noah's ("bring the flood-date"), Hūd's, Ṣāliḥ's, and Quraysh's to Muḥammad ﷺ. The demand *seems* reasonable (prophets *should* know futures?) yet smuggles three fallacies. *Category*: prophets are *warners*, not *calendars* — sent to *prepare* (v. 6: *tundhira*, warn), not *schedule*. *Epistemology*: Hour-knowledge belongs *exclusively* to God — *"knowledge thereof lies only with my Lord. None save He shall manifest it at its proper time"* (7:187) — demanding it of prophets is demanding divinity's prerogative from humanity's servants (v. 15's "but human" weaponized oppositely: *prove* non-humanity by date-knowing!). *Morality*: timing-knowledge would *harm* ( computed repentance — sin-till-eve, repent-at-deadline — insincerity scheduled), while timing-*ignorance* *helps* (readiness-always — v. 49's *"while they dispute"* suddenness *requires* perpetual preparation). The Prophet ﷺ answered timing-demands with readiness-redirects: asked *"when is the Hour?"*, he replied, *"What hast thou prepared for it?"* (al-Bukhārī 3681; Muslim 2639, from Anas) — *matā* (when) answered by *mādhā* (what-prepared). *That* is heaven's timetable-policy: dates concealed, readiness commanded.

**"This Promise": Naming the Resurrection as Waʿd**

*"This promise"* (*hādhā al-waʿd*): the deniers name resurrection *promise* — revealingly. *Waʿd* (promise/threat): the Day *promised* (to believers as hope, vv. 55–58) and *threatened* (to deniers as doom, vv. 59–64). Ibn Kathīr notes the irony: deniers call it *"this promise"* as if *confident* of good outcome (*"they thought they would be rewarded"* — cf. 42:18: *"those who believe not seek to hasten it"* — hastening as *mock*-eagerness: "bring our *reward* early!"). The Qur'an records the hastening-scoff: *"they seek to hasten the punishment"* (22:47; 29:53–54 — *"and Hell shall surely encompass disbelievers"*). *"This"* (*hādhā*): nearness-feigned — *this* (present, examinable) promise — yet they treat it as *that* (distant, dismissible). The demonstrative convicts: the promise *is* near (*"the Hour is near,"* 54:1; *"what will make thee know? Perhaps the Hour is nigh,"* 42:17) — nearer than jugular-vein (50:16's Nearness), nearer than morning (v. 49's suddenness). Their *"this"* (meant dismissively) testifies truly: *this*-near, *this*-certain, *this*-coming. The sūrah's Cries (vv. 29, 49, 51–53) *are* the promise's installments: town-Cry (past-prototype), world-Cry (future-certain), raising-Cry (final-gathering). *Matā* (when) will be answered by *ṣayḥah* (cry) — suddenly (v. 49), finally (v. 53). Ask *when*; receive *now* (unprepared). Better: prepare *now*; receive *then* (ready).

**"If You Are Truthful": Truthfulness Misdefined**

*"If you are truthful"* (*ṣādiqīn*): truthfulness (*ṣidq*) redefined as *date-knowing* — the town's error (v. 15: humanity-disqualifies) inverted (humanity-must-prove-superhuman). True *ṣidq* (truthfulness) is *message*-accuracy (warning true, promise certain), not *schedule*-specificity. Prophets are *ṣādiqūn* (truthful) in *what* (resurrection *will* come), not *when* (its hour *concealed*). The Qur'an defends the definition: *"the message bearers spoke true"* (v. 52 — the dead's confession: *ṣadaqa al-mursalūn* — truthfulness *vindicated* by arrival, not by advance-scheduling). Noah was *ṣādiq* (flood came, dateless-warned); the town's three *ṣādiqūn* (Cry came, vv. 28–29); Muḥammad ﷺ *al-ṣādiq* (Badr came, Makkah opened, Hour nears). Demanding *matā* (when) as *ṣidq*-proof confuses *nubuwwah* (prophethood: warning) with *kihānah* (soothsaying: forecasting) — prophets *warn* (prepare!), soothsayers *date* (entertain!). Islam *abolished* date-dealing (fortune-telling *ḥarām* — Muslim 2230: *"whoever visits a fortune-teller... "*), *commanded* preparation (*"prepare for it,"* above). The scoffers wanted prophets-as-soothsayers; God sent prophets-as-warners. *Ṣādiqīn* (truthful) are heeded-by-preparing, not tested-by-dating. Test them rightly — prepare — and their *ṣidq* manifests (v. 52's graveyard-confession, too late for mockers, timely for thee-now).

**The Hour's Concealment as Mercy and Wisdom**

Why *is* timing concealed? Revelation's wisdom is manifold. *Mercy*: known-dates breed strategic sinning (repentance scheduled, sincerity faked) and paralyzing dread (countdown-terror crushing life) — concealment preserves *sincere* striving (goodness for Goodness, not deadline) and *livable* hope (each day livable, each breath usable). *Justice*: suddenness (v. 49: *"while they dispute"*) *tests* readiness-fidelity — the always-ready (v. 11: Reminder-followers, unseen-fearers) pass; the deadline-awaiters fail. *"It shall come upon you but suddenly"* (7:187) — suddenness as examination-format. *Universality*: individual Hours (deaths) differ — each soul's *matā* (when) personalized-concealed — collective Hour's concealment mirrors personal: *both* sudden, *both* readiness-demanding. The Prophet ﷺ generalized: *"When one of you dies, his resurrection has begun"* (cited widely; cf. al-Bukhārī's *janāʾiz*-themes) — personal *waʿd* (promise) nearer than collective. *Pedagogy*: concealment *teaches* trust (*tawakkul* — outcomes to God) and urgency (*musāraʿah* — *"hasten to forgiveness,"* 3:133) together — the runner's (v. 20) *saʿy* (running) as timing-ignorance's fruit: *because* Cry-unknown, *therefore* run-now. Known-dates would walk; unknown-dates *run*. The sūrah *runs* its readers (vv. 20, 51's rushing) precisely by *matā*-concealing. *Blessed* concealment — it *runs* the wise, *ruins* only mockers (v. 49's disputing-seized).

**Answering "When?" With Readiness: Practices of the Prepared**

The verse converts *matā* (when) into *mādhā* (what-prepared). *Prepare decisively*: audit readiness — testimony current (v. 25's *āmantu* living), prayers established (five-daily *muḥḍar*-rehearsals, v. 32), debts settled (v. 29's no-bequest warning), repentance fresh (nightly *istighfār*), will written (Ibn ʿUmar's two-nights, v. 29). The prepared fear no *matā* (dates irrelevant to the ready); the unprepared fear every tick. *Prepare perpetually*: readiness-as-lifestyle — *"be ready"* each dusk (v. 37's flaying as death-rehearsal), each dawn (raising-rehearsal), each dispute (v. 49: Cry *while disputing* — reconcile quickly: *"do not dispute"* unto suddenness). The Prophet's ﷺ perpetual-readiness: sleeping with *duʿāʾ* (dying-words nightly), waking with *ḥamd* (resurrection-words daily), traveling with wills. *Redirect questioners*: when asked (or self-asked) *"when?"* (Hour? death? relief? victory?), answer prophetically — *"what hast thou prepared?"* — converting curiosity to inventory. Teach children: *Hour-unknown-therefore-kindness-now*. *Hope eagerly*: the promise (*waʿd*) is *promise* (hope!), not only threat — believers *await* (*"expecting,"* 9:52) meeting (v. 58's *"Peace!"*), vision (75:22–23: *"faces gazing at their Lord"*), reunion (52:21's families rejoined). *Matā* (when) on believers' lips is *longing* ( *"when* shall we meet?" — lovers' when), on mockers' *scoff*. Ask *lovers'*-when: *"when* wilt Thou accept?" — and *run* (v. 20) toward the Answer. For the promise *comes* — suddenly (v. 49), certainly (v. 53), sweetly-for-ready (vv. 55–58). *Matā*? *Soon* (54:1's *iqtarabat*, drawn-near). *Ready*? *Now* — God willing.

---
## Sūrah Yā Sīn 36:49

> **They await naught but a single cry that will seize them while they dispute among themselves,**

**Expanded Commentary**

**Mā Yanẓurūna Illā Ṣayḥatan: Awaiting — Nothing But Cry**

*"They await naught but"* — *mā yanẓurūna illā* — *mā... illā* (naught... but): deniers' *entire* future-expectation inventoried — *one* item. *Yanẓurūna* (await, expect, look-for): their *awaiting* (mock-*matā*, v. 48: "when?") answered: *thou awaitest* (whether mockingly or heedlessly) — *ṣayḥatan wāḥidah* (a single Cry). The verb's irony: they *await* (as if neutral observers) what will *seize* (as helpless victims) — spectatorship-illusion shattered. *"A single cry"* (*ṣayḥatan wāḥidatan*): the town's Cry (v. 29: *ṣayḥah wāḥidah* — identical phrase!) now cosmic: what extinguished *a town* (v. 29) will extinguish *the world*. The first blast of Seraphiel's trumpet (39:68: *"the Trumpet will be blown, and all in heavens and earth will swoon save whom God wills"*) — *"which will level all that is on the earth"* (source commentary). *Wāḥidah* (single): no second needed — creation's unmaking in one utterance (v. 82's *"Be!"* reversed). The deniers demanded *matā* (when, v. 48); heaven answers *mā* (what): *when*-unknown, *what*-certain — Cry. Await dates? Await *this*. The mockers' timetable-mongering (v. 48) meets inventory-reality: thy future holds *one* appointment (Cry), *one* sequel (raising, v. 51), *one* verdict (v. 54). *Yanẓurūna* (await) — heedlessly. *Taʾkhudhuhum* (seize) — helplessly. Between: *while disputing* — absurdly.

**Taʾkhudhuhum: Seizure — Sudden, Total, Inescapable**

*"That will seize them"* — *taʾkhudhuhum* — *akhdh* (seizing, grabbing, taking): violent apprehension — the Cry *grasps*. The verb's Qur'anic pedigree is destruction's: Pharaoh *seized* (7:130's years-of-punishment; 69:10: *"He seized them with exceeding seizure"*), towns *seized* (11:102: *"such is thy Lord's seizing when He seizes towns"*), Quraysh warned of *seizing* (16:45–47: *"do the plotters feel secure from God's seizing... while turning about, while fearful?"*). Seizure's features: *suddenness* (unwarned-grab), *totality* (whole-seized — persons, possessions, plans), *inescapability* (no outrunning sound). *"It will come while they are oblivious — disputing, bargaining, eating, drinking, engaging in worldly affairs"* (source commentary): oblivion-as-setting — seizure's *shock* maximized by mundanity (not mid-battle but mid-bargain!). The Prophet ﷺ detailed the mundanity: *"The Hour will come while two men spread a garment between them — they will not complete the sale nor fold it. While a man carries his camel's milk — he will not taste it. While a man mends his cistern — he will not water from it. While a man raises his morsel — he will not eat it"* (al-Bukhārī 6506; Muslim 2954, from Abū Hurayrah — the Hour's freeze-frame). Seizure mid-morsel: life's film stopped, frame burned. *Taʾkhudhuhum* — mid-raise, mid-chew, mid-deal. *Seized.*

**Wa Hum Yakhiṣṣimūn: "While They Dispute" — The Absurd Setting**

*"While they dispute among themselves"* — *wa hum yakhiṣṣimūn* — *yakhiṣṣimūn* (disputing, quarreling, litigating — mutual *khiṣām*): markets haggling, courts arguing, assemblies debating, families feuding, theologians polemicking (mock-*matā*, v. 48, *itself* a dispute!), nations warring-words. The Cry interrupts *speech* — disputes *about* (prices? prestige? the Hour's date?) silenced mid-syllable. The setting's absurdity is the verse's sting: eternity's Blast ends temporality's bickering — hagglers haggled-over-nothing (wealth *matāʿ-ilā-ḥīn*, v. 44, expired), litigants' cases mooted (all cases transferred to *the* Court, vv. 32, 53), debaters' points pointless (only *one* point proven: Cry-came). *Yakhiṣṣimūn* (mutual-form): disputes *require* partners — Cry-orphans all: no respondent left to answer, no audience to impress. The Qur'an's dispute-futility stands behind: *"they dispute concerning God without knowledge"* (22:8); *"dispute not, lest ye lose heart"* (8:46). The sūrah's disputers (vv. 15, 18, 47, 48, 77–78: humanity-objection, stoning-threats, feeding-scoff, timing-mock, bones-question) all *yakhiṣṣimūn* — and all will be *maʾkhūdhūn* (seized). Dispute's wages: seizure-mid-sentence. Counsel's alternative (v. 20's runner *pleaded*, not disputed; vv. 22–25 reasoned, not wrangled): plead truth; never *yakhiṣṣim* over it. Disputes end; Cries come. *Be pleading (or praying, giving, reconciling) — not disputing — when seized.*

**The Town's Cry and the World's: Precedent and Prospect**

The verse's *ṣayḥah wāḥidah* (single Cry) deliberately echoes verse 29's — precedent (town) guaranteeing prospect (world). The echo's logic: *same* God (Cry-capable), *same* instrument (single utterance), *same* suddenness (unwarned), *same* setting (mundanity — town mid-threats, v. 18; world mid-disputes) — *greater* scale (town → cosmos). Deniers-who-accept-history (towns fell — v. 31's ruins *seen*) must accept prospect (world *will* — Cry *comes*): precedent *proves* capacity, prospect *requires* readiness. The Qur'an's Cry-sequence completes: town-Cry (v. 29, past), world-Cry (this verse, future-first-blast), raising-Cry (vv. 51–53, future-second — *"the trumpet will be blown... they will rush"*). Three Cries, one Voice (*kun*, "Be!" — v. 82): unmaking-town, unmaking-world, remaking-all. Between first and second lies *now* (respite, v. 44: *ilā ḥīn*); between second and third, forty (years? — tradition: interval between blasts, v. 51's *"forty years,"* Ālūsī/Ibn Kathīr/Qurṭubī/Ṭabarī). *Now* is inter-Cry mercy: town-Cry warns (precedent-displayed), world-Cry nears (prospect-certain). The wise live *inter-Cry-ly*: past-Cry pondered (v. 31's *iʿtibār*, lesson-taking), future-Cry prepared (this verse's readiness), present-Cry-absent thanked (v. 35's *shukr*). *Inter-Cry* — mercy's address. Reside gratefully; depart (at Cry) ready.

**Living Seizable: Practices of the Dispute-Free**

The verse's *yakhiṣṣimūn* (disputing) becomes a lifestyle-warning with disciplines. *Dispute minimally*: shun quarrels (markets, courts, councils, comments-sections, family-feuds) — the Prophet ﷺ: *"No people stray after guidance but that disputation is given them"* (al-Tirmidhī 3263; Ibn Mājah 48, from Abū Umāmah; graded ṣaḥīḥ) — then recited *"they cite him only for disputation"* (43:58). Resolve fast (*"the best are swift reconcilers"* — cf. 49:9–10's peacemaking-duty); forgive faster (v. 26's praying-while-stoned). *Imagine seizure-settings*: before each dispute (argument heating, lawsuit filing, feud nursing), visualize *taʾkhudhuhum* (seizure) *wa-hum-yakhiṣṣimūn* (mid-dispute) — Cry freezing thy face mid-snarl, thy case mid-pleading, thy grudge mid-nursing. Perspective *dissolves* disputes: will this matter *ṣayḥah*-side? (Most won't.) *Occupy seizeable-moments nobly*: since seizure-timing is unknown, *sanctify* all timings — dispute-hours converted (to *dhikr*, service, reconciliation, earning-halal), idle-hours filled (Qur'an, family, charity), sin-hours abandoned (repent — seized-mid-sin = worst *yakhiṣṣimūn*). The righteous predecessors' motto: *"live each hour as if Cry-next"* — not morbidity but *nobility*. *Warn disputers lovingly*: when witnessing *yakhiṣṣimūn* (feuding kin, litigating neighbours, polemicizing brethren), counsel gently — *"shall the Cry seize us thus?"* — peacemake (49:10), remind (v. 45's *qīla*, it-is-said). For the verse's final mercy is its *yanẓurūna* (awaiting): Cry *awaits* (future), seizure *pending* — disputes *still* abandonable, readiness *still* achievable. *Await* — but *readily*. The Cry comes *while disputing* for disputers; for runners (v. 20), rejoicers (v. 55), and repenters — it comes *while ready*. *Be ready — not disputing — when seized.*

---
## Sūrah Yā Sīn 36:50

> **And then they can make no bequest, nor return to their people.**

**Expanded Commentary**

**Fa-Lā Yastaṭīʿūna Tawṣiyah: No Bequest Possible**

*"And then they can make no bequest"* — *fa-lā yastaṭīʿūna tawṣiyatan* — *lā yastaṭīʿūn* (they cannot, lack power): the Cry (v. 49) *paralyzes* agency. *Tawṣiyah* (bequest, exhortation, enjoined counsel): the deathbed's two functions — *disposing* (wealth-will: who gets what) and *exhorting* (moral-will: "fear God, pray, forgive"). The Cry cancels both: wealth undisposed (fortunes frozen mid-ownership), counsel unspoken (wisdom dying untransmitted). The verse's terror is *instantaneity*: *"The Hour will come upon people in an instant, so that they cannot attend to any affairs of this world"* (source commentary) — no sickbed-interval (days of farewells), no testament-moment (lawyers summoned), no *shahādah*-whisper (testimony prompted). The Prophet's ﷺ freeze-frames (v. 49: garment unfolded, milk un-tasted, cistern un-watered, morsel uneaten — al-Bukhārī 6506) extend here: *wills unwritten, advice ungiven*. *Tawṣiyah*'s second sense (*exhort/give advice*) yields the verse's implicit command (source commentary): *"there must be no delay in following the command to exhort one another to truth, and exhort one another to patience"* (103:3) — *exhort now*, for Cry-hour exhorts none. Every delayed counsel ("I'll advise him later," "I'll reconcile tomorrow") gambles against *fa-lā yastaṭīʿūn* (then-no-power). *Power* is *now*; *then* is powerless. *Bequeath — today.*

**Wa Lā Ilā Ahlihim Yarjiʿūn: Nor Return to People**

*"Nor return to their people"* — *wa lā ilā ahlihim yarjiʿūn* — *ahl* (people, family, household): the Cry severs *belonging*. The trader (mid-market, v. 49's disputes) returns not to shop/home; the traveler (mid-road) not to destination; the father (mid-errand) not to children; the son (mid-play) not to mother. *Yarjiʿūn* (return): return's *impossibility* — distance uncrossable (not miles but *Cry*), reunion unhoped (until *muḥḍarūn*, vv. 32, 53 — reunion-as-arraignment, not embrace). The clause's poignancy is domestic: life's sweetest verb (*return* — homecomings, embraces, "I'm back!") *negated*. The Qur'an's separation-gallery confirms: *"on that Day man flees from his brother, his mother, his father, his wife, his sons"* (80:34–36) — Cry-separation ripening into Judgment-flight. Against this, believers' *return* (to God — v. 83: *"unto Whom you shall be returned"*) consoles: earthly *ahl* (people) unreturned-to, heavenly *Ahl* (Garden's people, v. 55) joined. The runner (v. 20) left *ahl* (outskirts-home) running — and *returned* not (martyred) — yet *returned* (to God, v. 26: *udkhul*, enter) triumphantly. *All* leave *ahl*; the wise leave *toward* (God), not merely *from* (world). *Return* — to Him — before return-to-them is cut.

**Instantaneity as Theology: The Hour's "Blinking" Nature**

The verse (with v. 49) establishes eschatological *instantaneity*: divine action unbound by deliberation-duration. *"The matter of the Hour is as the blinking of an eye, or nearer still"* (16:77 — cited on v. 53). *"It shall come upon you but suddenly"* (7:187). The town's Cry (v. 29: *ṣayḥah wāḥidah*, single) previews; the world's Cry (v. 49) performs; this verse *details* (no-bequest, no-return as suddenness's *proofs*). Theologically, instantaneity displays *qudrah* (power): deliberation is weakness's need (men need time; God *says* — v. 82: *"Be!" and it is*). No angelic committees, no procedural delays — *utterance* executes. For deniers, instantaneity *terrifies* (no last-minute repentance — Pharaoh's drowning-*īmān* rejected, 10:91; v. 52's graveyard-confession unavailing). For believers, it *exhilarates* (no prolonged dying — martyrs' instant-*udkhul*, v. 26; righteous' swift-relief). Al-Rāzī notes instantaneity's *justice*: suddenness surprises only the *unready-by-choice* — the ready (v. 11: Reminder-followers) are *always* bequeathed (wills written, counsel given, returns made) — suddenness *finds* them finished. Unreadiness is *refusal* (of respite, v. 44: *ilā ḥīn* — time *was* given), not misfortune. *Ḥīn* (while) granted; *tawṣiyah* (bequest) neglected; Cry arrived. *Whose fault?* The verse asks silently — and answers: *theirs* (v. 54: *"save for what you did"*).

**Bequests and Returns: The Verse's Two Commandments**

The verse's negations (*no* bequest, *no* return) invert into present-tense *affirmations*: *bequeath now; return now*. *Bequeath*: Islam's will-law (*waṣiyyah*) implements the verse — *"prescribed when death approaches: bequest for parents and kin in fairness"* (2:180); *"after bequest or debt"* (4:11–12 — bequests settled *before* inheritance). The Prophet's ﷺ two-nights rule (v. 29: *"not right to spend two nights without written will"* — al-Bukhārī 2738) *is* this verse's fiqh: Cry-uncertainty mandates will-readiness. Write: debts (owed/owing), trusts (held), bequests (third-maximum, non-heirs), counsel (to children: faith, prayer, unity — Jacob's deathbed-*tawṣiyah*, 2:133: *"what will you worship after me?"*). Update regularly; store accessibly; witness properly. *Return*: reconcile *now* — visit parents (before Cry-cuts-visits), mend kinships (before *yarjiʿūn*-negated), forgive debtors, apologize offenders, embrace children. The Prophet ﷺ: *"whoever believes in God and Last Day, let him join kinship"* (al-Bukhārī 6138) — *joining* as Cry-proofing. Each night's home-return (*salām* upon entering — 24:61) rehearses; each farewell (*"I entrust you to God"*) finalizes. *Bequeathed* (will ready) and *returned* (ties mended) — the Cry finds such ones *finished*. *Finish — before finished.*

**Living Bequeathed and Returned: Daily Closure Practices**

The verse ritualizes *closure* as lifestyle. *Nightly bequest*: before sleep (v. 37's lesser-death), close accounts — forgive (all who wronged today — *"I have forgiven"*), seek forgiveness (*istighfār* for today's sins), settle intentions (tomorrow's *tawṣiyah*-targets), utter testimony (*shahādah* as sleep's bequest — dying-words nightly). Sleep *bequeathed* (will-current, heart-clear) — Cry-in-sleep (dawn-unraised) finds thee *ready*. *Daily returns*: return to *ahl* (people) *well* — enter homes with *salām* (peace, not grievance), leave with *duʿāʾ* (supplication, not coldness), call distant-kin regularly (voice-returns bridging miles). Mend one tie weekly (letter, visit, gift, apology) — *yarjiʿūn* (returns) banked against *lā-yarjiʿūn* (no-return). *Perpetual counsel*: exhort (103:3's *tawāṣaw*, mutual-exhortation) *continually* — advise children daily (brief faith-doses), counsel friends promptly (stings un-delayed), teach publicly (knowledge as *tawṣiyah*-ongoing, v. 12's *ṣadaqah jāriyah*). *Never* postpone counsel ("later" = Cry-gamble). For the verse's final mercy is its *fa-* (then): *then* (Cry-time) no-power — *now* (reading-time) full-power. *Then* seals; *now* opens. Wills writable *now*; returns runnable *now*; counsel speakable *now*. *Then* — silence. The runner (v. 20) ran *then*-available; the martyr counseled *then*-possible (vv. 20–25); his *tawṣiyah* (testimony, v. 25) outlived Cry (recited eternally). *Bequeath like him — counsel unto stones — return (to God) running.* *Fa-lā* (then-no) comes; *fa-ʿmal* (then-act) precedes. *Act.*

---
## Sūrah Yā Sīn 36:51

> **And the trumpet will be blown. Then, behold, they will rush forth from their graves unto their Lord.**

**Expanded Commentary**

**Wa Nufikha Fī al-Ṣūr: The Trumpet's Second Blowing**

*"And the trumpet will be blown"* — *wa nufikha fī al-ṣūr* — *nufikha* (blown): passive — the Blower (Seraphiel, *Isrāfīl* — trumpet-angel, lips at horn since creation, awaiting command) unnamed, the *blowing* (event) named. *Al-ṣūr* (trumpet/horn): the resurrection-instrument. This is the *second* blowing (the first: v. 49's world-ending Cry — *"which will level all"*; this: raising — *"will herald the Resurrection"*). Between them, tradition holds *"an interval of forty years"* (al-Alūsī, Ibn Kathīr, al-Qurṭubī, al-Ṭabarī — forty's unit debated: years most-held; the *ṣaḥīḥ* ḥadīth: *"between the two blowings, forty"* — Abū Hurayrah declined specifying days/months/years, al-Bukhārī 4935; Muslim 2955). During the interval: silence (creation swooned, 39:68: *"all in heavens and earth will swoon"*), decay (bodies dusted save tailbone — *"all of man decays save the tailbone,"* Muslim 2955), rain (forty-days' downpour sprouting bodies like plants — *"then God sends rain, and they sprout as herbs,"* al-Bukhārī 4935). Then: *nufikha* (blown!) — second. The Qur'an's trumpet-sequence stands behind: *"the Trumpet blown: all swoon save willed; then blown again: behold, they stand, beholding"* (39:68–70 — the relation-commentary). First blowing *unmakes* (v. 49's seizure); second *remakes* (this verse's rushing). Same horn, opposite harvests: *sown*-in-death, *reaped*-in-life. *Listen* — the lips are pursed; the command nears.

**Fa-Idhā Hum Mina al-Ajdāthi: "Behold, From Graves"**

*"Then, behold, they will rush forth from their graves"* — *fa-idhā hum mina al-ajdāthi* — *fa-idhā* (then, behold!): sudden-disclosure (v. 29's town-ash, v. 37's darkness, v. 51's rising — *idhā*-moments punctuating the sūrah). *Al-ajdāth* (graves, tombs): specifically *grave-pits* — dust-holes become birth-canals. The Qur'an's grave-emptying elsewhere: *"when graves are overturned"* (82:4); *"brings forth what is in graves"* (100:9). *Hum* (they): the *jamīʿ* (all, v. 32) — town's ash (v. 29), generations (v. 31), disputers (v. 49), runners (v. 20), prophets, Pharaohs — *all* grave-tenants evicted. The *idhā* (behold) summons *witnesses*: angels behold (marshaling), believers behold (confirmed-hope), deniers behold (themselves-rising! — v. 52's *"who raised us?!"*). No grave *holds*: marble mausoleums split, ocean-graves surface, ash-scattered re-gather (v. 79: *"He knows every creation"* — omniscience as reassembly-blueprint). The Prophet ﷺ: *"men will be raised barefoot, naked, uncircumcised"* (v. 32's ḥadīth, al-Bukhārī 6527) — grave-clothes shed, shrouds burst. *Ajdāth* (pits) yield *ajsād* (bodies): pits emptied, plains filled (*"a level plain, no crookedness,"* 20:106–107 — gathering-ground graded). *Behold* — earth's pockets turned out; humanity spilled; graves gaping, emptied, obsolete. *Rising.*

**Yansilūn: Rushing — Resurrection's Velocity**

*"They will rush forth"* — *yansilūn* — *nasalān* (rushing, hurrying, pouring-forth): rapid streaming — like water breaching, crowds stampeding. The verb recurs for Gog-Magog's swarm (*"from every hill they rush,"* 21:96) — multitudes in motion. Resurrection's *haste* is purposeful: *ilā rabbihim* (unto their Lord — destination drawing). The Qur'an's rushing-gallery: *"they will rush toward the Caller, heads raised"* (14:43; 54:8: *"rushing toward the Caller"*); *"as if rushing to a target"* (70:43 — racers to goals); *"moth-scattered"* (101:4 — then *locust-spread*, 54:7 — insect-images of number-and-agitation). *Why* rushing? *Compulsion* (the Caller *drives* — 50:21: *"every soul comes with a driver and witness"*), *terror* (the Scene *propels* — 70:43's humbled-eyes-down), *eagerness* (believers' *hope* hastens — meeting loved-Lord, v. 58's *"Peace!"*). Deniers rush *fearfully* (v. 52's woe-cry mid-rush); believers *joyfully* (*"their light runs before them,"* 66:8 — illumination escorting). Same *nasalān* (rushing), opposite hearts. The runner (v. 20: *yasʿā*, running) previews believers' rush: earthly running-toward-truth becomes eschatological rushing-toward-Lord — runners run *still*. *Yansilūn* — graves emptied at sprint. *Run now (v. 20); rush then — lightly.*

**Ilā Rabbihim: "Unto Their Lord" — Destination Divine**

*"Unto their Lord"* — *ilā rabbihim* — *ilā* (unto, toward): direction. *Rabbihim* (their Lord): Owner, Master — *theirs* (whether acknowledged, v. 22's *rabbikum*, or denied, v. 15). All rush *Lordward*: believers willingly (*"return to thy Lord, pleased, pleasing,"* 89:28), deniers unwillingly (*"driven to Hell in troops,"* 39:71 — Lordward-via-judgment). The destination *defines* resurrection: rising is *unto* (purpose), not merely *from* (graves). Corpses could reanimate meaninglessly; *ilā-rabbihim* (Lordwardness) means *reckoning* (v. 54: *"recompensed for what you did"*), *reunion* (v. 58's greeting), *requital* (vv. 55–64's abodes). Al-Rāzī notes *rabbihim* (Lord, not King/Judge): even judgment is *Lordship* — nurturing-Originator (v. 22's *faṭara*) completing nurture (rewarding/punishing as nurture's crown). Deniers meet the Lord they denied *as* Lord — recognition forced (*"our Lord, we have seen and heard — return us!"* 32:12 — sight-after-blindness, vv. 8–9). The verse's *ilā* (unto) also comforts the bereaved (Yā Sīn's graveside-recitation, Introduction): thy dead rush *Lordward* — toward Mercy (v. 5's *Raḥīm*), not loss. Graves are *ajdāth* (pits) *from* which (departure), the Lord *ilā* Whom (arrival). *Mourn* departure; *hope* arrival. *Lordward* — all rush. *Thy* rush: willing (now-believing) or unwilling (then-seeing)? *Choose willing — now.*

**Rushing Ready: Practices of the Grave-Bound**

The verse makes graves *starting-blocks* — train. *Visit starting-blocks*: graves regularly (v. 31's discipline — *"visit graves, they remind"*; stand at *ajdāth* reciting this verse: *hence I rush*). Picture *thy* pit: shroud, darkness, questioning (Munkar-Nakīr — al-Tirmidhī 1071), then *nufikha* (blown!) — *train* responses (Lord? Religion? Prophet? — rehearsed in life, answered in grave). *Lighten for rushing*: racers shed weight — shed sins (repentance-lightening), debts (settled), grudges (forgiven), doubts (studied-out). *"Barefoot, naked"* (ḥadīth) — only deeds clothe (*"provision: taqwā is best,"* 2:197). Pack *nūr* (light — 66:8's running-light): faith (lamp), prayer (*nūr*, Muslim 223), Qur'an (light-guide). *Run Lordward now*: *ilā-rabbihim* (unto-Lord) rehearsed daily — five prayers (Lordward-sprints), night-prayer (solitude-rushes), charity (wealth sent-Lordward, v. 12's *qaddamū*), counsel (runners' *saʿy*, v. 20). The earthly-Lordward become eschatological-Lordward seamlessly: practiced-rushers rush gladly; practiced-fleers (from prayer, charity, truth) rush *dragged* (50:21's driver). *Long for the Blowing*: believers *await* (not dread) *nufikha* — meeting (v. 58), vision (75:23), reunion (52:21). The Prophet ﷺ: *"whoever loves meeting God, God loves meeting him"* (al-Bukhārī 6507; Muslim 2683, from ʿUbādah). Love the Meeting — then *nufikha* (blown) is wedding-trumpet, *yansilūn* (rushing) wedding-procession, *ilā-rabbihim* (Lordward) homecoming. *Graves* — bridal-chambers. *Blow, horn — Thy runners wait.*

---
## Sūrah Yā Sīn 36:52

> **They will say, "Oh, woe unto us! Who has raised us from our place of sleep?" "This is that which the Compassionate did promise; and the message bearers spoke true."**

**Expanded Commentary**

**Yā Waylanā: The Woe-Cry of the Raised**

*"Oh, woe unto us!"* — *yā waylanā* — *wayl* (woe, ruin, destruction): perdition's exclamation — Hell's *own* word (*"woe to the falsifiers,"* 83:1; *"woe to every slanderer,"* 104:1). *Yā* (O): grief's vocative — the dead *address* their ruin. The speakers: deniers (rushed, v. 51 — rushing-while-wailing). Their *first* resurrection-words are *lament*, not praise — guilt's reflex: the conscious-stricken *know* (v. 54's recompense deserved) before sentence pronounced. The alternate reading (*waylanā min baʿthinā* — "woe for our being-raised," Ālūsī/Qurṭubī) sharpens: woe *at* resurrection itself — existence-resumed as calamity (better unraised! — *"would that I were dust!"* 78:40). Contrast believers' first-words: praise (*"praise be to God who removed grief,"* 35:34), peace-greeted (*"peace upon you — enter,"* 39:73). First-words reveal hearts: woe-filled (denial's harvest) versus praise-filled (faith's). The Prophet ﷺ warned of woe-earners: hoarders (*wayl*), pelatih prayer-neglecters (*wayl*, 107:4), traders-swearing-falsely. *Wayl* accumulates pre-grave; *yā-waylanā* erupts post-raising. *Audit* woe-sources now (each *wayl*-verse a mirror) — lest thy first eternal words be *woe*. The runner's (v. 20) first words-after-death were *yā-layta* (would-that-my-people-knew, v. 26 — longing-love); deniers': *yā-waylanā* (woe-us — despair-hate). *First-words* are *last-lives'* echo. *Live* praise-bound; *rise* praise-voiced.

**Man Baʿathanā Min Marqadinā: "Who Raised Us From Sleep?"**

*"Who has raised us from our place of sleep?"* — *man baʿathanā min marqadinā* — *man* (who?): bewilderment's interrogative — raisers unknown (God unacknowledged — v. 15's denial persisting past death!). *Baʿathanā* (raised/sent us): resurrection as *sending* (mission-like — raised *for* purpose: arraignment, vv. 32, 53). *Marqad* (sleeping-place, bed): death-as-sleep — *"from our sleep"* (alternate, Ālūsī): the grave a *bed*, death a *nap*. The metaphor's mercy-and-terror: *mercy* — the dead *rested* (punishment-of-grave excepted — deniers' *marqad* uneasy, yet *lighter* than Fire-after; believers' *marqad* paradisal — *"garden of Paradise's gardens,"* al-Tirmidhī 2460); *terror* — sleepers *startled* (trumpet-alarm mid-nap — *"as if they had tarried but an hour,"* 10:45 — millennia felt as siesta!). The question (*man*, who) answers itself (as v. 22's *wa-mā-liya*): *He* (the Compassionate-Promiser, v. 52b; the Originator, v. 22; the Cry-Crier, vv. 29, 49). Deniers ask *who* (feigned-ignorance); the verse answers *what* (promise-true, messengers-true). *Marqad* (bed) also indicts: beds *imply* risings (sleepers wake!) — death-as-sleep *promises* resurrection-as-morning. They *named* (sleep) what *proves* (waking) — self-convicted by metaphor. *Every* night's *marqad* (bed) preached (v. 37: sleep-death, dawn-raising); *every* morning's *baʿth* (waking) proved. Proof-slept-in, proof-doubted. *Who raised?* — the Waker of ten thousand dawns. *Ask* — and *know* (too late for askers, timely for thee).

**"This Is That Which the Compassionate Promised": Promise Kept**

*"This is that which the Compassionate did promise"* — *hādhā mā waʿada al-Raḥmān* — the *answer* (by angels, Ibn Kathīr — or deniers'-own realization): *this* (resurrection, beheld) *is that* (promised, v. 48's *waʿd* — mocked *"when?"* — *now*!). *Al-Raḥmān* (the Compassionate): Promise-Keeper named *tenderly* — even in wrath's dawn, mercy-titled (v. 5's *Raḥīm*, v. 15's *Raḥmān*-acknowledged-yet-denied). The deniers' *matā* (when, v. 48) is answered not with *date* but *event*: *when* = *now* (behold!). *"It does not matter when the promise comes, only that people be prepared"* (source commentary) — preparedness, not scheduling, was *ever* the issue. The promise's *Compassionate*-sourcing consoles believers (resurrection-as-mercy: reunion, reward, vision — vv. 55–58) and convicts deniers (mercy-warned-yet-spurned — v. 45's *laʿallakum-turḥamūn*, mercy-offered). *Hādhā* (this): demonstrative of *presence* — promise no-longer-future but *here* (graves-emptied, v. 51; Lord-neared, v. 51's *ilā-rabbihim*). Every *"when?"* (v. 48) meets its *"this!"* (v. 52): scoff-then, sight-now. Scoffers demanded timetable; timetable arrives as *trumpet*. *Better* timetable-accepted (prepare-now, vv. 48–50) than trumpet-answered (woe-then). *This* — prepare. *That* — promised. *Now* — warning. *Then* — woe-or-welcome (vv. 55–64). *Choose welcome.*

**Wa Ṣadaqa al-Mursalūn: "The Messengers Spoke True"**

*"And the message bearers spoke true"* — *wa ṣadaqa al-mursalūn* — vindication's verdict: *ṣadaqa* (spoke-truly, proved-true) — the town's three (vv. 14–17: called liars, v. 15's *takdhibūn*), Noah (mocked, 11:38), Muḥammad ﷺ (*ṣādiq* denied) — *all* true. The confessors: deniers (self-convicted — *"we were wrong,"* 21:14's town-cry) and/or angels (prosecution-confirmed). *Ṣadaqa* (past): truth *established* — debate closed, appeal none. The phrase crowns the sūrah's truth-theme: Wise Qur'an *true* (v. 2), Prophet *sent-true* (v. 3), warning *true* (v. 6), Word-due *true* (v. 7), messengers *true* (here) — truth's chain unbroken, denial's snapped. The confession's *tardiness* is its tragedy: truth-acknowledged post-Cry avails naught (Pharaoh's 10:91-rejected; 6:158's *"belief benefits not"* post-signs). *Ṣadaqa*-then (compelled, beheld) ≠ *ṣadaqa*-now (chosen, unseen — v. 11's *bi-al-ghayb*, unseen-faith). Value lies in *pre*-sight confession (faith, 2:3) — post-sight is compulsion (even devils confess-then, 7:38's mutual-cursing). The verse thus *begs* pre-Cry *ṣadaqa*: *say now* (messengers true!) what thou *wilt* say then — *now* saves, *then* seals. Testify (v. 25's *āmantu*) pre-trumpet; confess (*ṣadaqa*) pre-grave. *Then*-confessors wail (*yā-waylanā*); *now*-confessors rejoice (v. 55's *fākihūn*). *Confess — now.*

**Waking Before the Waking: Practices of the Pre-Raised**

The verse's graveyard-scene becomes a drill. *Rehearse rising*: visit graves (v. 31) reciting vv. 51–52 *as* the dead: *"who will raise me?"* — answer: *the Compassionate-Promiser* (faith), *messengers-true* (conviction). Sleep nightly as *marqad*-practice (bed-as-grave, waking-as-*baʿth*): evening *duʿāʾ* (*"in Thy name I die"*), morning *ḥamd* (*"who gave life after death"* — v. 37's liturgy). Each dawn: mini-v.52 (*raised-from-marqad* — praise, not woe!). *Convert woe-to-warning*: list *wayl*-verses (83:1 falsifiers, 104:1 slanderers, 107:4 prayer-neglecters, 51:10 conjecturers) — audit self post-each: *am I wayl-bound?* Amend (measure fairly, guard tongue, pray timely, verify beliefs). *Woe*-then is *warning*-now heeded. *Confess messengers-true now*: testify daily (*shahādah* with *ṣadaqa*-conviction — Muḥammad-true, Qur'an-true, resurrection-true — v. 48's *waʿd* certain). Teach children *ṣadaqa al-mursalūn* as catechism (prophets-true, all!). Counter mock-*matā* (v. 48's "when?") with comfort-*hādhā* (this-comes; prepare). For the verse's final mercy is its *marqad* (bed): death-as-*sleep* (restful-word!) — even deniers *rested* (relatively) pre-raising; believers *repose* (gardens-of-graves, ḥadīth). Sleep *now* (nightly) peacefully (bequeathed, v. 50; returned, v. 50; confessed, here) — and rise *then* (v. 51) *praising* (35:34), not wailing (*yā-waylanā*). *Marqad* — bed. *Baʿth* — morning. *Ṣadaqa* — confessed. *Welcome* — prepared. *Sleep well — rise well.*

---
## Sūrah Yā Sīn 36:53

> **There shall be but a single cry. Then, behold, they will all be arraigned before Us!**

**Expanded Commentary**

**In Kānat Illā Ṣayḥatan Wāḥidah: The Third Single Cry**

*"There shall be but a single cry"* — *in kānat illā ṣayḥatan wāḥidatan* — *in... illā* (naught... but): the sūrah's *third* ṣayḥah-wāḥidah (town's, v. 29; world's, v. 49; gathering's, here) — identical phrasing, ascending scale: town-extinguished (v. 29), world-seized (v. 49), all-arraigned (here). The parallels confirm: *"it is but a single cry"* (79:13–14 — same gathering-Cry). *"That it is but a single cry is said to imply that the matter of the Hour is as the blinking of an eye, or nearer still"* (16:77 — source commentary): divine dispatch defies duration — utterance-assembles-billions. *Which* Cry is this? The raising-gathering (vv. 51–52's trumpet *effect*: blown, v. 51 → rushed, v. 51 → wailed/confessed, v. 52 → *arraigned*, here). One Cry (v. 49) *ends* (world); one blast (v. 51) *raises*; one Cry (here) *gathers* — or two blasts (39:68) encompassing three effects — numbering varies, singularity constant: *wāḥidah* (single) thrice-insisted. Repetition *is* the theology: God does not *labour* (legions, v. 28-denied; procedures, delays) — He *utters*. Creation: *"Be!"* (v. 82). Destruction: *ṣayḥah* (Cry). Re-creation: *ṣayḥah*. Judgment: *kun*-like summons (*"come!"*). Four utterances frame existence: *Be — Cry — Cry — Come*. *Hear* the first three (recited); *prepare* the fourth (awaited). *Wāḥidah* — one suffices. *One* sufficed town (v. 29); *one* suffices cosmos. *Power* speaks briefly.

**Fa-Idhā Hum Jamīʿun Ladaynā Muḥḍarūn: All Arraigned**

*"Then, behold, they will all be arraigned before Us!"* — *fa-idhā hum jamīʿun ladaynā muḥḍarūn* — *fa-idhā* (then, behold!): disclosure (v. 51's rising *beheld*, now gathering *beheld*). *Jamīʿ* (all): totality (v. 32's *kull... jamīʿ* — each-and-all). *Ladaynā* (before Us): nearest-Presence (v. 32's intimacy-terror). *Muḥḍarūn* (arraigned/produced): compelled-presence (v. 32's judicial-passive). The verse *is* verse 32 fulfilled: v. 32 *promised* (*"each shall be arraigned"*); v. 53 *performs* (*"behold, all arraigned!"*). Promise-to-performance arc spans the sūrah: warned (vv. 6–11), recorded (v. 12), parabled (vv. 13–30), signed (vv. 33–44), counseled (vv. 45–47), scoffed (v. 48), Cried (v. 49), un-bequeathed (v. 50), trumpeted (v. 51), confessed (v. 52) — *arraigned* (here). *Muḥḍarūn!* — the exclamation-mark implicit: *produced!* Defendants delivered; docket complete; Judge presiding. No absconders (mountains fled-to? — v. 43's *lā-ṣarīkha*, none-answering; 69:28's *"my authority has perished"*), no delays (16:77's blinking-swiftness), no substitutions (6:164: *"no bearer bears another's burden"*). *Jamīʿ* (all): prophets *and* peoples (mutual-witnessing — 4:41: *"We bring thee as witness over these"*), kings *and* outcasts (runner, v. 20, beside his king), rich *and* Abū-Bakr-fed-poor (v. 47's fed-testifying). *All* — behold!

**The Cry-to-Court Continuum: Judgment's Mechanics**

The verse compresses judgment's *mechanics*: Cry → presence. No transit-delay (rushing, v. 51, *is* transit — sprint-graves-to-court), no waiting-room (assembly-plain *is* courtroom — 20:106's level-plain), no preparation-time (preparation was *pre*-Cry, vv. 48–50). The Qur'an's courtroom-procedure unfolds: *production* (here; vv. 32), *book-distribution* (69:19, 25: right/left hands), *witness-calling* (limbs, v. 65; earth, 99:4; prophets, 4:41; God suffices, 13:43), *weighing* (101:6–9: scales-heavy/light), *sentencing* (vv. 55–64's abodes). This verse stages *production* — the docket's completion. Its *behold!* (*idhā*) addresses three audiences: angels (marshals — *"drive"* orderly, 50:21), believers (vindicated-watchers — *"this is what was promised!"* 36:52-realized), deniers (docketed-defendants — v. 52's woe-continued). Al-Rāzī notes production's *publicity*: arraignment *jamīʿ* (all-together) — no private-hearings (concealers exposed — *"that Day secrets are tried,"* 86:9), no character-witnesses-summoned-by-defense (limbs *prosecute*, v. 65). Public-production *is* partial-punishment (shame pre-Fire) and partial-reward (honour pre-Garden — *"their light runs,"* 66:8, publicly-radiant). *Muḥḍarūn* — produced. *Ladaynā* — transparently. *Jamīʿ* — totally. *Court* — convened. *Next* (v. 54): verdict-principles announced.

**Why One Cry Suffices for Billions: Omnipotence's Arithmetic**

The verse's *wāḥidah* (single) gathering *jamīʿ* (all-billions) displays omnipotence's *non*-arithmetic: human gathering scales with effort (ushe*rs*, venues, days for thousands); divine with *utterance* (one Cry for all-ever-lived). The Qur'an states the scaling-law: *"Your creation and your raising are but as a single soul"* (31:28) — one-or-billions, equal-ease. *"Our command is but one, as the twinkling of sight"* (54:50) — command singular, effect total. The denier's arithmetic ("*how* gather dust-scattered-billions?" — v. 78's bones-question writ-large) dissolves: gathering is not *collecting* (piece-by-piece) but *summoning* (*"come!"* — pieces *obey*). Obedience scales effortlessly: one word, all-dust-heeds. The town (v. 29) proved micro-scale (one Cry, one town); the verse promises macro (one Cry, all). *Proof* (town-ash, v. 29-beheld; ruins, v. 31-visited) underwrites *promise* (all-arraigned, here-awaited). Believers' arithmetic inverts: *my* raising (one soul) is *easier* than all-yet-equally-certain — no soul *lost* in crowds (*"We counted all in registry,"* v. 12 — crowding confuses clerks, not the Counter). *Jamīʿ* (all) comforts (none forgotten — *"thy Lord never forgets,"* 19:64) and warns (none overlooked — *"weight-of-atom seen,"* 99:7–8). *One* Cry; *all* produced; *each* judged (v. 54: *"no soul wronged"*). *Arithmetic* — divine.

**Awaiting Production: Practices of the Soon-Produced**

The verse's *muḥḍarūn* (produced) becomes a preparation-program. *Produce thyself pre-Production*: voluntary *iḥḍār* (self-production) — nightly self-arraignment (v. 32's *muḥāsabah*: stand, review, repent — bed-as-dock, conscience-as-prosecutor, mercy-as-defense-hoped). *Pre*-produced (self-judged) face *re*-production lightly (*"call yourselves to account before accounting"* — v. 32's ʿUmar-maxim). *Dress for production*: *muḥḍarūn* arrive *as-lived* — deeds as garments (*"garments of righteousness,"* 7:26, best). Clothe presently: faith (undergarment), prayer (daily-dress), charity (cloak), character (adornment). Strip presently: sins (stains), grudges (stench), hypocrisy (costume — *"secrets tried,"* 86:9, un-costumes). *Rehearse responses*: production *questions* (grave's three — Lord? Religion? Prophet? — extend to Court's five: *"feet move not till asked of life, knowledge, wealth-source/spending, body"* — al-Tirmidhī 2417, from Ibn Masʿūd; graded ṣaḥīḥ) — prepare answers *by living them* (life-for-God, knowledge-acted, wealth-halal-spent, body-obeyed). *Witness-well presently*: production *witnesses* (limbs, v. 65; earth, 99:4) record *now* — employ witnesses-righteously (tongue-*dhikr*, hands-giving, feet-mosque-ward, v. 12; earth-multiplied-prayer-spots). For the verse's final mercy is its *fa-idhā* (then-behold): *beholding* precedes *being-beheld* — thou *beholdest* (this verse, now) before *art-beheld* (arraigned, then). *Behold* — and *become* beholdable (presentable, *ladaynā*-worthy). Cry-single comes; crowds-assemble; Court-convenes. *Produced* — willing (now-ready) or driven (then-rushed, v. 51)? *Produce thyself — now — willingly.* *Muḥḍarūn* — ready.

---
## Sūrah Yā Sīn 36:54

> **This day no soul will be wronged in any way, and you will not be recompensed, save for that which you used to do.**

**Expanded Commentary**

**Lā Tuẓlamu Nafsun Shayʾan: The Charter of Non-Wronging**

*"This day no soul will be wronged in any way"* — *fa-al-yawma lā tuẓlamu nafsun shayʾan* — *al-yawma* (this Day): Judgment-Day (arraignment-Day, vv. 32, 53). *Lā tuẓlamu* (not wronged): passive — wronging *done-to-none*. *Nafsun* (soul): *any* soul — believer/disbeliever, prophet/people, king/outcast (v. 53's *jamīʿ*, all). *Shayʾan* (anything/at-all): *no* wronging *of any kind* — neither inflated-punishment (more than deserved) nor deflated-reward (less than earned), neither unattributed-good (uncredited) nor misattributed-evil (another's sin). The Qur'an's justice-charter resounds: *"Judgment will be made between them in truth, and they shall not be wronged"* (39:69); *"you shall not be wronged"* (45:22; 46:19). *"This verse does not mean none shall be punished; rather, punishment is because they wronged themselves"* (source commentary): Hell is not *God-wronging-souls* but *souls'-self-wronging* harvested — *"God wrongs them not, but themselves do they wrong"* (3:117); *"God does not wrong men at all, but men wrong themselves"* (10:44). The *tuẓlamu* (wronged)-negation thus *affirms* responsibility: sentences *fit* (v. 54b: *"save for what you did"*), scales *balance* (101:6–9), books *match* (18:49: *"finds present"*). *Al-yawma* (This-Day) contrasts *al-dunyā* (this-world — wrongings rife: tyrants unpunished, righteous wronged): world-injustice *temporary* (appeal-pending), Day-justice *final* (appeal-none — *"no injustice today,"* 40:17). *Wronged-now?* — Day-rights. *Wronging-now?* — Day-reckons. *Lā-tuẓlam* — guaranteed.

**Wa Lā Tujzawna Illā Mā Kuntum Taʿmalūn: Recompense Fitting Deeds**

*"And you will not be recompensed, save for that which you used to do"* — *wa lā tujzawna illā mā kuntum taʿmalūn* — *lā... illā* (not... save): recompense *restricted* to deeds. *Tujzawna* (recompensed): *jazāʾ* (requital — good-for-good, ill-for-ill). *Mā kuntum taʿmalūn* (what you used to do): habitual-doing (*kuntum*, used-to — lifestyle, not lapses). The principle: *wages match work* — Garden for faith-acted (vv. 55–58), Fire for denial-acted (vv. 59–64). The Qur'an's wage-law: *"whoever does an atom's good sees it; whoever evil's atom sees it"* (99:7–8); *"today each soul is recompensed for what it earned"* (40:17). *Kuntum* (used-to) nuances: judgment weighs *patterns* (used-to-pray/give/forgive versus used-to-deny/mock/withhold), not *moments* (lapses repented-erased, 25:70's replacement; deathbed-exceptions, Pharaoh-excluded, 10:91). The second-person (*tujzawna*, *you*-recompensed) *addresses* (deniers primarily — the sūrah's warned; believers secondarily — reassured): *your* wages, *your* work. No favoritism (lineage — *"no kinship then,"* 23:101; wealth — *"neither wealth nor sons avail,"* 26:88; crowds — *"each comes alone,"* 19:95). *Mā-taʿmalūn* (what-you-do) *is* destiny's currency: spenders (v. 47) *paid* (v. 11's *ajr-karīm*); withholders *charged* (vv. 63–64's *burn*); runners (v. 20) *crowned* (vv. 26–27); stoners *Cried* (v. 29). *Do* — wages follow. *Guaranteed.*

**Justice's Apologetic: Answering "Cruel God" Charges**

The verse (with 3:117, 10:44) is revelation's theodicy-center, answering suffering-and-Hell's challenge: *how can a Merciful (v. 5's Raḥīm) punish?* The answer's strands: *self-wronging* (punishment as *harvest*, not *infliction* — *"themselves do they wrong,"* 3:117 — the sick-by-lifestyle analogy: lungs *self*-smoked, judgment *self*-earned). *Full-warning* (punishment post-proclamation — vv. 6, 14–17, 20–25, 45 — sentence-after-summons, Cry-after-counsel). *Proportionality* (punishment *fitting* — *"save for what you did"* — no excess-atom; mercy *exceeding* — good repaid ten-to-700-fold, 6:160; 2:261 — evil *single*, good *multiplied*: generosity-asymmetry favoring sinners!). *Freedom* (deeds *chosen* — v. 7's *fa-hum-lā-yuʾminūn*, for-they-believe-not — will's fruit, not decree's force). *Opportunity* (respite *granted* — v. 44's *ilā-ḥīn*, enjoyment-while; repentance *open-till-death* — 39:53's *"despair not"*). *Five* mercies precede one sentence: warned, respited, out-proportioned (good-multiplied), freed, forgiven-if-turning. *Then* sentenced — *fittingly*. *"Cruel"* charges ignore all five. The analogy: teacher warns (syllabus), teaches (year-long), offers-extra-credit (good-multiplied), frees (study-or-not), forgives-late-work (repentance) — then *fails* the blank-paper (v. 50's un-bequeathed, v. 49's disputing-seized). *Cruel*? — or *just*? The verse verdicts: *just* (*lā-tuẓlam*, no-wronging). *Blank-papers* fail *themselves*. *Study* (v. 11: follow-Reminder) — *pass* (vv. 55–58).

**"This Day" Versus Every Other: Temporality of Justice**

*Al-yawma* (This Day) contrasts all *other* days: world-days (injustice-possible — witnesses lie, judges err/bribed, powerful evade) versus Day (injustice-*impossible* — Witness-Omniscient, v. 76; Judge-Infallible; Powerful-leveled, 69:29's authority-perished). The contrast *consoles* the world-wronged: courts-failed? — Court-awaits (*"God will judge between you on Resurrection Day,"* 2:113). Oppressor-triumphed? — triumph-*matāʿ-ilā-ḥīn* (v. 44: enjoyment-while — *while* expires). Rights-denied? — *"the hornless sheep's right taken from horned"* (Muslim 2582, from Abū Hurayrah — animal-justice(!) — *a fortiori* human). The contrast *warns* the world-wronging: evasion-then-impossible (limbs-testify, v. 65; earth-reports, 99:4; books-match, 18:49); power-then-useless (26:88's wealth/sons-unavailing; 69:28–29's perished-authority); intercession-then-permission-only (20:109; 2:255 — no strings-pulled). *Al-yawma* also *individualizes*: *"no soul"* (*nafsun* — each-soul) — crowds no-cover (nations-judged-soul-by-soul — 19:95's *kulluhum-ātihi-fardan*, each-comes-alone). Family-ties severed-as-shields (80:34–36's fleeing; 31:33's parent/child-unavailing). *Thy* Day: *thou* (soul), *thy* book (v. 12's registry-excerpt), *thy* limbs (v. 65's witnesses), *thy* Lord (v. 51's *ilā-rabbihim*). *Alone* — yet *unwronged* (*lā-tuẓlam*). Solitude guaranteed; injustice *impossible*. *Prepare* solitude (deeds-company — *"three follow the dead... deeds remain,"* al-Bukhārī 6514); *trust* non-wronging. *Al-yawma* — ready?

**Living Unwronging and Unwronged: Day-Ready Justice**

The verse's justice-charter becomes a conduct-code. *Wrong none*: since Day-rights *all*, *pre*-right (restore *now* — debts paid, slanders retracted, wages rendered, feelings mended). The Prophet's ﷺ Day-economics: *"whoever wronged (brother's honour/property), let him settle today before currency-less Day (deeds-traded)"* (al-Bukhārī 6534, from Abū Hurayrah — *qaṣāṣ*-by-deeds: good-taken, evil-loaded). Audit weekly: *whom wronged?* (family? employees? neighbours? self-by-sin?) — restore promptly. *Wronging* includes self (sins — 3:117's self-wronging): repent (un-self-wrong). *Bear wrongs patiently* (without surrendering rights): world-wronged? — options: forgive (virtue — *"whoever pardons... reward with God,"* 42:40), claim-lawfully (courts, *qiṣāṣ* — 2:178), or defer-to-Day (*"God suffices,"* 4:45 — Day's *lā-tuẓlam* as patience's bank). Never *avenge*-wrongfully (excess-retaliation = fresh-wronging, 16:126's like-for-like-limit). *Do requitable-good*: since *tujzawna-mā-taʿmalūn* (recompensed-for-doing), *do* (prayer, charity, counsel, kindness — v. 12's *qaddamū*, sent-forth, as Day-deposits). * habitualize* (*kuntum*, used-to — patterns, not spasms): daily-Qur'an (habit), daily-charity (habit — even smile, al-Tirmidhī 1956), daily-forgiveness (habit). For the verse's final mercy is its *fa-* (then/therefore): *fa-al-yawma* (therefore-This-Day) — Day-justice *follows* (life-opportunity, vv. 6–53) *logically*: warned (vv. 6–11) → recorded (v. 12) → parabled (vv. 13–30) → signed (vv. 33–44) → Cried (v. 49) → raised (v. 51) → *therefore*-judged-justly. Logic-complete; mercy-complete; justice-complete. *Lā-tuẓlam* (unwronged) — promised. *Mā-taʿmalūn* (thy-doing) — thine. *Do well — be well-recompensed.* *Guaranteed.*

---
## Sūrah Yā Sīn 36:55

> **Truly the inhabitants of the Garden on that Day will be busy rejoicing,**

**Expanded Commentary**

**Inna Aṣḥāba al-Jannati: The Garden's Inhabitants Introduced**

*"Truly the inhabitants of the Garden"* — *inna aṣḥāba al-jannati* — *inna* (truly): emphasis-certainty (v. 54's justice *executed* — Garden *populated*). *Aṣḥāb* (inhabitants/companions): *ṣuḥbah* (companionship) — Garden-dwellers as *companions* (of Garden, of one another, of God — 4:69's *"goodly company"*: prophets, truthful, martyrs, righteous). *Al-jannah* (the Garden): Paradise (earthly-*jannāt*, v. 34, capitalized-eternal). *"On that Day"* (*al-yawma* — *fī shughulin*, "on that Day in occupation"): Day-context (v. 54's *al-yawma*, This-Day) — Garden-life *Day*-inaugurated (judgment-morning → eternity). The verse *opens* the blessed-contrast (vv. 55–58: Garden) before wretched-contrast (vv. 59–64: Fire) — mercy-before-wrath (v. 5's *Raḥīm* preceding judgment; 6:54's mercy-prescribed). Order *pastoral*: hope-before-fear (believers heartened-first, deniers warned-after). The *aṣḥāb*-phrasing echoes town-parable's *aṣḥāb-al-qaryah* (v. 13: town's people) — *aṣḥāb*-to-*aṣḥāb*: town-companions Cried (v. 29), Garden-companions rejoicing (here) — companionship's *object* decides companions' *end*. Befriend town (denial) → Cry; befriend Garden-path (faith) → rejoicing. *Choose companions* (messengers, v. 21; runners, v. 20; Reminder, v. 11) — companionship *is* destiny. *Aṣḥāb-al-jannah* — enrolled how? *Believing-unseen* (v. 11), *following* (v. 21), *running* (v. 20), *testifying* (v. 25). *Enroll — now.*

**Fī Shughulin Fākihūn: Busy — Rejoicing**

*"Will be busy rejoicing"* — *fī shughulin fākihīn/fākihūn* — *shughul* (occupation, business, engagement): *busy-ness*. *Fākihūn* (rejoicing, delighted, merry — *fakihah* also fruit/enjoyment): *joyful*-busy-ness. Paradox: *busy* (world-busy = stressed, hurried, burdened) + *rejoicing* (joy-busy = absorbed, delighted,PLAYful) — Garden-business as *absorption-in-delight*. *"Busy with delights of Paradise and spiritual realities"* (Ibn Kathīr, al-Qurṭubī): occupations: feasting (v. 57's fruit), reclining (v. 56), conversing (52:25's mutual-questioning), beholding (v. 58's greeting; 75:23's vision), exploring (*"no soul knows what delights hidden,"* 32:17). *Fākihūn* (rejoicing) contrasts world-*shughul* (anxious-busyness — v. 49's *yakhiṣṣimūn*, disputing-busy; 102:1's rivalry-diverted) and Fire-*shughul* (v. 64's *burn*-busy — torment-occupied). Three busynesses: world (distracted), Fire (tormented), Garden (delighted). *"Those in different levels occupied with different delights"* (al-Qurṭubī): ranks varied (prophets', martyrs', righteous' — 4:69's company-tiers), joys tailored (*"whatsoever they call for,"* v. 57 — personalized-bliss). Highest: *"absorbed in vision of God"* (al-Rāzī — *ruʾyah*, beholding — 75:22–23; *"looking"* as supreme-shughul). *Busy* — eternally (no boredom — bliss-novelty infinite, 32:17's *hidden*-unexhausted). *Rejoicing* — effortlessly (no joy-*work* — delight-as-breathing). *World*-busy? — redirect (busy-ness *for* Garden: prayer, charity, counsel — *shughul*-transferred). *Garden*-busy — promised.

**Occupation Versus Preoccupation: Garden Psychology**
